## Economy

Main article: [Economy of New York City](3848717.md)



The [New York Stock Exchange](21560.md)



The [Financial District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_District,_Manhattan) of [Lower Manhattan](455646.md), seen from [Brooklyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn)

Manhattan is the economic engine of New York City, with its 2.45 million workers drawn from the entire [New York metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_metropolitan_area) accounting for approximately half of all jobs in New York City.[^cite_note-BLS2023Q3-214] At US\$1.007 trillion in 2024,[^cite_note-ManhattanGDP-8] Manhattan (New York County) had the [highest total GDP as well as GDP per capita of any county in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._counties_by_GDP). Manhattan's workforce is overwhelmingly focused on [white collar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-collar_worker) professions. In 2010, Manhattan's daytime population was swelling to 3.94 million, with [commuters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commuter) adding a net 1.48 million people to the population, along with visitors, tourists, and commuting students. The commuter influx of 1.61 million workers coming into Manhattan was the largest of any county or city in the country.[^cite_note-215] Anchored by Manhattan's financial institutions, New York City has been described as the [financial capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_centre) of the world.[^cite_note-NYCFinancialAndFintechCapitalWorld-216]

### Financial sector

Main article: [Wall Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street)

Manhattan's most important economic sector lies in its role as the headquarters for the [U.S. financial industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_center), metonymously known as Wall Street. Manhattan is home to the [New York Stock Exchange](21560.md) (NYSE), at [11 Wall Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/11_Wall_Street) in Lower Manhattan, and the [Nasdaq](21559.md), now located at [4 Times Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4_Times_Square) in Midtown Manhattan, representing the world's largest and second-largest [stock exchanges](28168.md), respectively, when measured both by overall share trading value and by total [market capitalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_capitalization) of their listed companies in 2023.[^cite_note-LargestExchanges-30] The [NYSE American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NYSE_American) (formerly the American Stock Exchange, AMEX), [New York Board of Trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Board_of_Trade), and the [New York Mercantile Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Mercantile_Exchange) (NYMEX) are also located downtown.

### Corporate sector

New York City is home to the most corporate headquarters of any city in the United States, the overwhelming majority based in Manhattan.[^cite_note-217] Manhattan had more than 520 million square feet (48 million square meters) of office space in 2022,[^cite_note-218] making it the largest office market in the United States; while [Midtown Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midtown_Manhattan), with more than 400 million square feet (37 million square meters) is the largest central business district in the world.[^cite_note-219] [Lower Manhattan](455646.md) is the third-largest U.S. central business district (following the [Chicago Loop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Loop)).[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221] New York City's role as the top global center for the [advertising industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advertising) is [metonymously](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metonymously) known as ["Madison Avenue"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madison_Avenue#Advertising_industry).[^cite_note-222]

### Tech and biotech

Further information: [Tech companies in Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tech_companies_in_Manhattan), [Biotech companies in Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biotech_companies_in_Manhattan), [Silicon Alley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Alley), and [Tech\:NYC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tech\:NYC)



The [Flatiron District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flatiron_District), the birthplace and center of [Silicon Alley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Alley)

[^cite_note-223]

Manhattan has driven New York's status as a top-tier global high technology hub.[^cite_note-ManhattanNowBiggestTechHub-224][^cite_note-NewYorkCityDestinationNumberOneTechHub-225] [Silicon Alley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Alley), once a [metonym](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metonym) for the sphere encompassing the metropolitan region's [high tech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_tech) industries,[^cite_note-226] is no longer a relevant moniker as the city's tech environment has expanded dramatically both in location and in its scope. New York City's current tech sphere encompasses a universal array of applications involving [artificial intelligence](1164.md), the internet, [new media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_media), [financial technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_technology) (*fintech*) and [cryptocurrency](36662188.md), [biotechnology](4502.md), [game design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Game_design), and other fields within information technology that are supported by its [entrepreneurship ecosystem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entrepreneurship_ecosystem) and [venture capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venture_capital) investments. As of 2014, New York City hosted 300,000 employees in the tech sector.[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-CNNMoney-228] In 2015, Silicon Alley generated over US\$7.3 billion in venture capital investment,[^cite_note-VentureCapitalNY1-229] most based in Manhattan, as well as in [Brooklyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn), [Queens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queens), and elsewhere in the region. High technology [startup companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Startup_companies) and employment are growing in Manhattan and across New York City, bolstered by the city's emergence as a global node of [creativity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Innovation) and [entrepreneurship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entrepreneurship),[^cite_note-VentureCapitalNY1-229] [social tolerance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_tolerance),[^cite_note-SocialToleranceNY1-230] and [environmental sustainability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainability),[^cite_note-EnvironmentalSustainabilityNY1-231][^cite_note-EnvironmentalSustainabilityNY2-232] as well as New York's position as the leading Internet hub and telecommunications center in North America, including its vicinity to several [transatlantic fiber optic trunk lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transatlantic_telephone_cable), the city's [intellectual capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intellectual_capital), and its extensive outdoor [wireless connectivity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wireless_network).[^cite_note-233] [Verizon Communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verizon_Communications), headquartered at [140 West Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/140_West_Street) in Lower Manhattan, was at the final stages in 2014 of completing a US\$3 billion [fiberoptic telecommunications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiberoptic_communication) upgrade throughout New York City.[^cite_note-234]

The [biotechnology](4502.md) sector is also growing in Manhattan based upon the city's strength in academic scientific research and public and commercial financial support. By mid-2014, Accelerator, a biotech investment firm, had raised more than US\$30 million from investors, including [Eli Lilly and Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eli_Lilly_and_Company), [Pfizer](62304.md), and [Johnson & Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnson_&_Johnson), for initial funding to create biotechnology startups at the Alexandria Center for Life Science, which encompasses more than 700,000 square feet (65,000 m<sup>2</sup>) on [East 29th Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/29th_Street_\(Manhattan\)) and promotes collaboration among scientists and entrepreneurs at the center and with nearby academic, medical, and research institutions. The [New York City Economic Development Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Economic_Development_Corporation)'s Early Stage Life Sciences Funding Initiative and venture capital partners, including [Celgene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celgene), [General Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric) Ventures, and Eli Lilly, committed a minimum of US\$100 million to help launch 15 to 20 ventures in [life sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_sciences) and biotechnology.[^cite_note-235] In 2011, Mayor [Michael R. Bloomberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_R._Bloomberg) had announced his choice of [Cornell University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornell_University) and [Technion-Israel Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technion-Israel_Institute_of_Technology) to build a US\$2 billion graduate school of [applied sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Applied_science) on [Roosevelt Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roosevelt_Island), Manhattan, with the goal of transforming New York City into the world's premier technology capital.[^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237]

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_New_York_City)



[Times Square](31389.md) is the hub of [Broadway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadway_theater)'s [theater district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theater_District,_Manhattan) and a major Manhattan cultural venue with 50 million tourists annually, making it one of the world's most popular tourist destinations.

[^cite_note-Ann_Shields-31]

Tourism is vital to Manhattan's economy, and the landmarks of Manhattan are the focus of New York City's tourists, with a record 66.6 million visiting the city in 2019, bringing in \$47.4 billion in tourism revenue. Visitor numbers dropped by two-thirds in 2020 during the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), climbing back to 63.3 million visitors in 2023.[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239]

According to [The Broadway League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Broadway_League), shows on [Broadway](725252.md) sold approximately US\$1.54 billion worth of tickets in the 2022–2023 and the 2023–2024 seasons with attendance of approximately 12.3 million each.[^cite_note-BroadwayLeagueStatistics-240]

### Real estate

Real estate is a major force driving Manhattan's economy. Manhattan has perennially been home to some of the world's most valuable real estate, including the [Time Warner Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Warner_Center), which had the highest-listed market value in the city in 2006 at US\$1.1 billion,[^cite_note-241] to be subsequently surpassed in October 2014 by the [Waldorf Astoria New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waldorf_Astoria_New_York), which became the most expensive hotel ever sold after being purchased by the [Anbang Insurance Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anbang_Insurance_Group), based in China, for US\$1.95 billion.[^cite_note-Robert_Frank-242] When 450 Park Avenue was sold on July 2, 2007, for US\$510 million, about US\$1,589 per square foot (US\$17,104/m²), it broke the barely month-old record for an American office building of US\$1,476 per square foot (US\$15,887/m²) based on the sale of 660 [Madison Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madison_Avenue).[^cite_note-243] In 2014, Manhattan was home to six of the top ten [ZIP Codes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ZIP_Code) in the United States by median housing price.[^cite_note-244] In 2019, the most expensive home sale ever in the United States occurred in Manhattan, at a selling price of US\$238 million, for a 24,000-square-foot (2,200 m<sup>2</sup>) [penthouse apartment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penthouse_apartment) overlooking [Central Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Park),[^cite_note-245] while [Central Park Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Park_Tower), topped out at 1,550 feet (472 m) in 2019, is the [world's tallest residential building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_residential_buildings), followed globally in height by [111 West 57th Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/111_West_57th_Street) and [432 Park Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/432_Park_Avenue), both also located in Midtown Manhattan.

### Media

Main articles: [Media in New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_New_York_City) and [New Yorkers in journalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Yorkers_in_journalism)

See also: [List of films set in New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_films_set_in_New_York_City) and [List of television shows set in New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_television_shows_set_in_New_York_City)



The headquarters of *[The New York Times](30680.md)* at 620 [Eighth Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eighth_Avenue_\(Manhattan\))

Manhattan has been described as the [media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_New_York_City) capital of the world.[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247] A significant array of media outlets and their journalists report about international, American, [business](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_journalism), [entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entertainment_journalism), and [New York metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_metropolitan_area)–related matters from Manhattan.

Manhattan is served by the major New York City daily [news publications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newspaper), including *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, which has won the most [Pulitzer Prizes](24230.md) for journalism[^cite_note-248] and is considered the U.S. media's [newspaper of record](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newspaper_of_record);[^cite_note-249] the *[New York Daily News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Daily_News)*; and the *[New York Post](102227.md)*, which are all headquartered in the borough. The nation's largest newspaper by circulation, *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)*, is also based in Manhattan.[^cite_note-250] Other daily newspapers include *[AM New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AM_New_York)* and *[The Villager](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Villager_\(Manhattan\))*. *[The New York Amsterdam News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_York_Amsterdam_News)*, based in Harlem, is one of the leading Black-owned weekly newspapers in the United States. *[The Village Voice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Village_Voice)*, historically the largest [alternative newspaper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_newspaper) in the United States, announced in 2017 that it would cease publication of its print edition and convert to a fully digital venture.[^cite_note-VillageVoiceDigital-251]

The television industry developed in Manhattan and is a significant employer in the borough's economy. The four major American broadcast networks, [ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Broadcasting_Company), [CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS), [NBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC), and [Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Broadcasting_Company),[^cite_note-252] as well as [Univision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Univision), are all headquartered in Manhattan, as are many cable channels, including [CNN](62028.md), [MSNBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MSNBC), [MTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV), [Fox News](11121.md), [HBO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HBO), and [Comedy Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comedy_Central). In 1971, [WLIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WLIB) became New York City's first Black-owned radio station[^cite_note-253] and began broadcasts geared toward the African-American community in 1949.[^cite_note-254] [WQHT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WQHT), also known as *Hot 97*, claims to be the premier hip-hop station in the United States.[^cite_note-255] [WNYC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNYC), broadcasting on both an AM and FM signal, has the largest [public radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_radio) audience in the nation and is the most-listened to commercial or non-commercial radio station in Manhattan.[^cite_note-256] [WBAI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WBAI), owned by the non-profit [Pacifica Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacifica_Foundation), broadcasts eclectic music, as well as political news, talk and opinion from a [left-leaning](18499.md) viewpoint.[^cite_note-257]

The oldest [public-access television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public-access_television) cable TV channel in the United States is the [Manhattan Neighborhood Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Neighborhood_Network), founded in 1971, offers eclectic local programming that ranges from a [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz) hour to discussions of labor issues to foreign language and religious programming.[^cite_note-258] [NY1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NY1), [Charter Communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charter_Communications)'s local news channel, is known for its beat coverage of City Hall and state politics.[^cite_note-259]

## Education

See also: [Education in New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_New_York_City), [List of high schools in New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_high_schools_in_New_York_City), and [List of colleges and universities in New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_colleges_and_universities_in_New_York_City)



[Stuyvesant High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuyvesant_High_School) in [Tribeca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribeca)

[^cite_note-260]

Education in Manhattan is provided by a vast number of public and private institutions. Non-charter public schools in the borough are operated by the [New York City Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Department_of_Education),[^cite_note-261] the largest public school system in the United States. Charter schools include [Success Academy Harlem 1 through 5, Success Academy Upper West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Success_Academy_Charter_Schools), and [Public Prep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Prep).

Several notable New York City public high schools are located in Manhattan, including [A. Philip Randolph Campus High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A._Philip_Randolph_Campus_High_School), [Beacon High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Beacon_School), [Stuyvesant High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuyvesant_High_School), [Fiorello H. LaGuardia High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiorello_H._LaGuardia_High_School), [High School of Fashion Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_School_of_Fashion_Industries), [Eleanor Roosevelt High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eleanor_Roosevelt_High_School_\(New_York_City,_New_York\)), [NYC Lab School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NYC_Lab_School), [Manhattan Center for Science and Mathematics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Center_for_Science_and_Mathematics), [Hunter College High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter_College_High_School), and [High School for Math, Science and Engineering at City College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_School_for_Math,_Science_and_Engineering_at_City_College). [Bard High School Early College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bard_High_School_Early_College), a hybrid school created by [Bard College](276259.md), serves students from around the city.

Many [private preparatory schools](612857.md) are also situated in Manhattan, including the [Upper East Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_East_Side)'s [Brearley School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brearley_School), [Dalton School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalton_School), [Browning School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Browning_School), [Spence School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spence_School), [Chapin School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapin_School), [Nightingale-Bamford School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nightingale-Bamford_School), [Convent of the Sacred Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convent_of_the_Sacred_Heart_\(New_York\)), [Hewitt School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hewitt_School), [Saint David's School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_David's_School_\(New_York_City\)), [Loyola School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loyola_School_\(New_York_City\)), and [Regis High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regis_High_School_\(New_York_City\)). The [Upper West Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_West_Side) is home to the [Collegiate School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collegiate_School_\(New_York_City\)) and [Trinity School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinity_School_\(New_York_City\)). The borough is also home to [Manhattan Country School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Country_School), [Trevor Day School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trevor_Day_School), [Xavier High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xavier_High_School_\(New_York_City\)) and the [United Nations International School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_International_School).

Based on data from the 2011–2015 [American Community Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Community_Survey), 59.9% of Manhattan residents over age 25 have a bachelor's degree.[^cite_note-262] As of 2005, about 60% of residents were college graduates and some 25% had earned advanced degrees, giving Manhattan one of the nation's densest concentrations of highly educated people.[^cite_note-263]



The notable architectural design of [Butler Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butler_Library) at [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University), an [Ivy League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivy_League) university in Manhattan

[^cite_note-264]

Manhattan has various colleges and universities, including [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University) (and its affiliate [Barnard College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barnard_College)), [Cooper Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooper_Union), [Marymount Manhattan College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marymount_Manhattan_College), [New York Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Institute_of_Technology), [New York University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_University) (NYU), [The Juilliard School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Juilliard_School), [Pace University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pace_University), [Berkeley College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berkeley_College), [The New School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_School), [Yeshiva University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeshiva_University), and a campus of [Fordham University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fordham_University). Other schools include [Bank Street College of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_Street_College_of_Education), [Boricua College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boricua_College), [Jewish Theological Seminary of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Theological_Seminary_of_America), [Manhattan School of Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_School_of_Music), [Metropolitan College of New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_College_of_New_York), [Parsons School of Design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parsons_School_of_Design), [School of Visual Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_of_Visual_Arts), [Touro College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Touro_College), and [Union Theological Seminary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Theological_Seminary). Several other private institutions maintain a Manhattan presence, among them [Adelphi University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adelphi_University), [Mercy University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercy_University), [King's College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King's_College_\(New_York_City\)), [St. John's University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._John's_University_\(New_York_City\)), and [Pratt Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pratt_Institute). [Cornell Tech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornell_Tech), part of [Cornell University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornell_University), is developing on [Roosevelt Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roosevelt_Island).

The [City University of New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_University_of_New_York) (CUNY), the municipal college system of New York City, is the largest urban university system in the United States, serving more than 226,000-degree students and a roughly equal number of adult, continuing and professional education students.[^cite_note-265] A third of college graduates in New York City graduate from CUNY, with the institution enrolling about half of all college students in New York City. CUNY senior colleges located in Manhattan include: [Baruch College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baruch_College), [City College of New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_College_of_New_York), [Hunter College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter_College), [John Jay College of Criminal Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Jay_College_of_Criminal_Justice) and [William E. Macaulay Honors College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_E._Macaulay_Honors_College); graduate studies and doctorate-granting institutions are [Craig Newmark Graduate School of Journalism at the City University of New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Craig_Newmark_Graduate_School_of_Journalism_at_the_City_University_of_New_York), [CUNY Graduate Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CUNY_Graduate_Center), [CUNY Graduate School of Public Health and Health Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CUNY_Graduate_School_of_Public_Health_and_Health_Policy), [CUNY School of Labor and Urban Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CUNY_School_of_Labor_and_Urban_Studies) and [CUNY School of Professional Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CUNY_School_of_Professional_Studies).[^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267] The only CUNY community college located in Manhattan is the [Borough of Manhattan Community College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borough_of_Manhattan_Community_College).[^cite_note-268] The [State University of New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_University_of_New_York) is represented by the [Fashion Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fashion_Institute_of_Technology), [State University of New York State College of Optometry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_University_of_New_York_State_College_of_Optometry), and [Stony Brook University – Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stony_Brook_Manhattan).[^cite_note-269]



[New York Public Library Main Branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Public_Library_Main_Branch) at [42nd Street](61068.md) and [Fifth Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth_Avenue)

Manhattan is a world center for training and education in medicine and the life sciences.[^cite_note-270] The city as a whole receives the second-highest amount of annual funding from the [National Institutes of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institutes_of_Health) among all U.S. cities,[^cite_note-271] the bulk of which goes to Manhattan's research institutions, including [Memorial Sloan-Kettering Cancer Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_Sloan-Kettering_Cancer_Center), [Rockefeller University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockefeller_University), [Mount Sinai School of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Sinai_School_of_Medicine), [Columbia University College of Physicians and Surgeons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University_College_of_Physicians_and_Surgeons), [Weill Cornell Medical College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weill_Cornell_Medical_College), and [New York University School of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_University_School_of_Medicine).

Manhattan is served by the [New York Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Public_Library), which has the largest collection of any public library system in the country.[^cite_note-272] The five units of the Central Library—[Mid-Manhattan Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-Manhattan_Library), [53rd Street Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/53rd_Street_Library), the [New York Public Library for the Performing Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Public_Library_for_the_Performing_Arts), Andrew Heiskell Braille and Talking Book Library, and the [Science, Industry and Business Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science,_Industry_and_Business_Library)—are all located in Manhattan.[^cite_note-273] More than 35 other branch libraries are located in the borough.[^cite_note-274]

## Culture

See also: [Culture of New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_New_York_City)

Further information: [Broadway theatre](725252.md), [LGBTQ culture in New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_culture_in_New_York_City), [Transgender culture of New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_culture_of_New_York_City), [List of museums and cultural institutions in New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_and_cultural_institutions_in_New_York_City), [Music of New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_New_York_City), [List of nightclubs in New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_nightclubs_in_New_York_City), [Met Gala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Met_Gala), [New York Fashion Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Fashion_Week), [NYC Pride March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NYC_Pride_March), and [Stonewall Riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stonewall_Riots)



The [Lincoln Center for the Performing Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Center_for_the_Performing_Arts)



The [Metropolitan Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Museum_of_Art)

Manhattan is the borough most closely associated with New York City by non-residents; residents within the [New York City metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_metropolitan_area), including New York City's boroughs outside Manhattan, will often describe a trip to Manhattan as "going to the City".[^cite_note-275] Poet [Walt Whitman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walt_Whitman) characterized the streets of Manhattan as being traversed by "hurrying, feverish, electric crowds".[^cite_note-276]

Manhattan has been the scene of many important global and American cultural movements. The [Harlem Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harlem_Renaissance) in the 1920s established the African-American literary canon in the United States and introduced writers [Langston Hughes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Langston_Hughes) and [Zora Neale Hurston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zora_Neale_Hurston). Manhattan's visual art scene in the 1950s and 1960s was a center of the [pop art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_art) movement, which gave birth to such giants as [Jasper Johns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasper_Johns) and [Roy Lichtenstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roy_Lichtenstein). The downtown pop art movement of the late 1970s included artist [Andy Warhol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Warhol) and clubs like [Serendipity 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serendipity_3) and [Studio 54](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Studio_54), where he socialized.

Broadway theater is considered the highest professional form of theater in the United States. Plays and musicals are staged in one of the 39 larger professional theaters with at least 500 seats, almost all in and around Times Square. [Off-Broadway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Off-Broadway) theaters feature productions in venues with 100–500 seats.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278] [Lincoln Center for the Performing Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Center_for_the_Performing_Arts), anchoring [Lincoln Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Square,_Manhattan) on the Upper West Side of Manhattan, is home to 12 influential arts organizations, including the [Metropolitan Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Opera), [New York Philharmonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Philharmonic), and [New York City Ballet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Ballet), as well as the [Vivian Beaumont Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vivian_Beaumont_Theater), the [Juilliard School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juilliard_School), [Jazz at Lincoln Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz_at_Lincoln_Center), and [Alice Tully Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice_Tully_Hall). Performance artists displaying diverse skills are ubiquitous on the streets of Manhattan.

Manhattan is also home to some of the most extensive [art collections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_collection) in the world, both [contemporary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_art) and [classical art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_art), including those on [Museum Mile on Fifth Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth_Avenue#Museum_Mile), such as the [Metropolitan Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Museum_of_Art), the [Frick Collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frick_Collection), the [Frank Lloyd Wright](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Lloyd_Wright)-designed [Guggenheim Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solomon_R._Guggenheim_Museum), the [Neue Galerie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neue_Galerie_New_York), and the [Jewish Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Museum). Other major museums in Manhattan include the [Whitney Museum of American Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitney_Museum_of_American_Art) and the [Museum of Modern Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Modern_Art) (MoMA). The Upper East Side has many art galleries,[^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280] and the downtown neighborhood of [Chelsea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chelsea,_Manhattan) is known for its more than 200 art galleries that are home to modern art from both upcoming and established artists.[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282] Many of the world's most lucrative [art auctions](2983120.md) are held in Manhattan.[^cite_note-ManhattanArtAuction1-283][^cite_note-ManhattanArtAuction2-284]





The [Empire State Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_State_Building) displays the colors of the [Rainbow Flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rainbow_flag) as an [LGBT icon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rainbow_flag_\(LGBT\)), top. The annual [NYC Pride March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NYC_Pride_March) in June (seen here in 2018) is the [world's largest LGBT event](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_LGBT_events), imaged below.

[^cite_note-NYCWorld'sLargestPrideParade-285]

[^cite_note-NYCWorld'sMediaCapitalLargestPrideParade-286]

Manhattan is the epicenter of [LGBTQ culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_culture_in_New_York_City) and the central node of the LGBTQ+ [sociopolitical ecosystem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_sociology).[^cite_note-NYCGayCapitalOfTheWorld1-287] The borough is widely acclaimed as the cradle of the modern [LGBTQ rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights) movement, with its inception at the 1969 [Stonewall riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stonewall_riots).[^cite_note-KentuckyStonewall-103][^cite_note-PinkNewsStonewall-288][^cite_note-EncycloStonewall-289][^cite_note-NPSStonewall-104][^cite_note-ObamaStonewall-23] Brian Silverman, the author of *Frommer's New York City from \$90 a Day,* wrote the city has "one of the world's largest, loudest, and most powerful LGBT communities", and "Gay and lesbian culture is as much a part of New York's basic identity as [yellow cabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_cab), [high-rise buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-rise_building), and Broadway theatre"—[^cite_note-290] radiating from this central hub, as [LGBT travel guide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_travel) *Queer in the World* states, "The fabulosity of Gay New York is unrivaled on Earth, and [queer culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_culture) seeps into every corner of its five boroughs".[^cite_note-291] Multiple [gay villages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_culture_in_New_York_City#Gay_villages) have developed, spanning the length of the borough from the [Lower East Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_East_Side), [East Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Village_\(Manhattan\)), and Greenwich Village, through Chelsea and [Hell's Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hell's_Kitchen), uptown to [Morningside Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morningside_Heights).

The annual [NYC Pride March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NYC_Pride_March) (or [gay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NYC_Pride_March) [pride parade](58168.md)) traverses southward down [Fifth Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth_Avenue) and ends at Greenwich Village; the Manhattan parade is the largest pride parade in the world, attracting tens of thousands of participants and millions of sidewalk spectators each June.[^cite_note-NYCWorld'sLargestPrideParade-285][^cite_note-NYCWorld'sMediaCapitalLargestPrideParade-286] [Stonewall 50 – WorldPride NYC 2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stonewall_50_%E2%80%93_WorldPride_NYC_2019) was the [largest international Pride celebration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_LGBT_events) in history, produced by [Heritage of Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heritage_of_Pride). The events were in partnership with the [I ❤ NY](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Love_New_York) program's LGBT division, commemorating the 50th anniversary of the Stonewall uprising, with 150,000 participants and five million spectators attending in Manhattan.[^cite_note-Authorities1-292]

The borough is represented in several prominent [idioms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idiom). The phrase *[New York minute](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/New%20York%20minute)* is meant to convey an extremely short time such as an instant,[^cite_note-NewYorkMinuteDefinition-293] sometimes in hyperbolic form, as in "perhaps faster than you would believe is possible," referring to the rapid pace of life in Manhattan.[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295] The expression "[melting pot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melting_pot)" was first popularly coined to describe the densely populated immigrant neighborhoods on the [Lower East Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_East_Side) in [Israel Zangwill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Zangwill)'s play *[The Melting Pot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Melting_Pot_\(play\))*, which was an adaptation of [William Shakespeare](32897.md)'s *[Romeo and Juliet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romeo_and_Juliet)* set in New York City in 1908.[^cite_note-296] The iconic [Flatiron Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flatiron_Building) is said to have been the source of the phrase "[23 skidoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/23_skidoo)" or scram, from what cops would shout at men who tried to get glimpses of women's dresses being blown up by the winds created by the triangular building.[^cite_note-297] The "[Big Apple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Apple)" dates back to the 1920s, when a reporter heard the term used by New Orleans stable-hands to refer to New York City's [horse racetracks](74711.md) and named his racing column "Around The Big Apple". [Jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz) musicians adopted the term to refer to the city as the world's jazz capital, and a 1970s ad campaign by the New York Convention and Visitors Bureau helped popularize the term.[^cite_note-298]









Clockwise, from upper left: the annual [Macy's Thanksgiving Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macy's_Thanksgiving_Day_Parade), the world's largest parade;

[^cite_note-NYCThanksgivingParadeLargestWorld-299]

&#x20;the annual [Halloween Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York's_Village_Halloween_Parade) in [Greenwich Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenwich_Village), the world's largest Halloween parade, with millions of spectators annually, and with its roots in [New York's queer community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_culture_in_New_York_City);

[^cite_note-NewYorkCityQueerHalloweenParade-300]

&#x20;the annual [Philippine Independence Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Independence_Day_Parade), the largest [outside Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipinos_in_the_New_York_metropolitan_area);

[^cite_note-NYCLargestPhilippineDayParadeOutsideManila-301]

&#x20;and the [ticker-tape parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ticker-tape_parades_in_New_York_City) for the [Apollo 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_11) [astronauts](664.md)

Manhattan is well known for its street [parades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parade), which celebrate a broad array of themes, including holidays, nationalities, human rights, and major league sports team championship victories. The majority of higher profile parades in New York City are held in Manhattan. The annual [Macy's Thanksgiving Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macy's_Thanksgiving_Day_Parade) is the world's largest parade,[^cite_note-NYCThanksgivingParadeLargestWorld-299] beginning alongside [Central Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Park) and processing southward to the flagship [Macy's Herald Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macy's_Herald_Square) store;[^cite_note-302] the parade is viewed on telecasts worldwide and draws millions of spectators in person.[^cite_note-NYCThanksgivingParade-303]

Other notable parades including the world's oldest [St. Patrick's Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_St._Patrick's_Day_Parade), held annually in March since 1762,[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305] the [Greenwich Village Halloween Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenwich_Village_Halloween_Parade) in October,[^cite_note-306] and numerous parades commemorating the independence days of many nations.[^cite_note-307] [Ticker-tape parades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ticker-tape_parades_in_New_York_City) celebrating victorious sporting championships as well as other national accomplishments march northward on [Broadway](4626.md) from [Bowling Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bowling_Green_\(New_York_City\)) to [City Hall Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Hall_Park) in Lower Manhattan, along the [Canyon of Heroes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canyon_of_Heroes).[^cite_note-308] [New York Fashion Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Fashion_Week), held at various locations in Manhattan, is a high-profile semiannual event featuring [models](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fashion_model) displaying the latest wardrobes created by prominent fashion designers worldwide.

## Sports



[Madison Square Garden](75129.md), home to the [New York Rangers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Rangers) of the [National Hockey League](21809.md) and the [New York Knicks](72855.md) of the [National Basketball Association](22093.md)

Manhattan is home to the [NBA](22093.md)'s [New York Knicks](72855.md) and the [NHL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NHL)'s [New York Rangers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Rangers), both of which play their home games at [Madison Square Garden](75129.md), the only major professional [sports arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_arena) in the borough.[^cite_note-309] The Garden was also home to the [WNBA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNBA)'s [New York Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Liberty) through the [2017 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_New_York_Liberty_season), but that team's primary home is now the [Barclays Center](1658814.md) in [Brooklyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn). The [New York Jets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Jets) proposed a [West Side Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Side_Stadium) for their home field, but the proposal was defeated in June 2005, and they now play at [MetLife Stadium](5476983.md) in [East Rutherford, New Jersey](136878.md).[^cite_note-310]

Manhattan does not currently host a [professional baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Professional_baseball) franchise. The original [New York Giants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_New_York_Giants_\(baseball\)) played primarily in the various incarnations of the [Polo Grounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polo_Grounds) from their inception in 1883 until they headed to California with the [Brooklyn Dodgers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn_Dodgers) after the 1957 season.[^cite_note-311] The New York Yankees began their [franchise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_New_York_Yankees) as the Highlanders, named for [Hilltop Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilltop_Park), where they played from their creation in 1903 until 1912.[^cite_note-312] The team moved to the Polo Grounds with the 1913 season, where they were officially christened the *New York Yankees*, remaining there until they moved across the [Harlem River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harlem_River) in 1923 to [Yankee Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yankee_Stadium_\(1923\)).[^cite_note-313] The [New York Mets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Mets) played in the Polo Grounds in 1962 and 1963, their first two seasons, before [Shea Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shea_Stadium) was completed in 1964.[^cite_note-314] After the Mets departed, the Polo Grounds was demolished in April 1964.[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316]

The first national college-level basketball championship, the [National Invitation Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Invitation_Tournament), was held in New York in 1938 and remains in the city.[^cite_note-317] The [New York Knicks](72855.md) started play in 1946 as one of the [National Basketball Association](22093.md)'s original teams, playing their first home games at the [69th Regiment Armory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/69th_Regiment_Armory), before making Madison Square Garden their permanent home.[^cite_note-318] The [New York Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Liberty) of the [WNBA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNBA) shared the Garden with the Knicks from their creation in 1997 as one of the league's original eight teams through the 2017 season,[^cite_note-319] after which the team moved nearly all of its home schedule to [White Plains, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Plains,_New_York).[^cite_note-320] [Rucker Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rucker_Park) in [Harlem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harlem) is a playground court, famed for its *[streetball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streetball)* style of play, where many NBA athletes have played in the summer league.[^cite_note-321]

Although both of New York City's football teams play today in [MetLife Stadium](5476983.md) in [East Rutherford, New Jersey](136878.md), both teams started out playing in the Polo Grounds. The [New York Giants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Giants) played side-by-side with their baseball namesakes from the time they entered the [National Football League](21211.md) in 1925, until crossing over to Yankee Stadium in 1956.[^cite_note-322] The New York Jets, originally known as the *Titans of New York*, started out in 1960 at the Polo Grounds, before joining the Mets in Queens at [Shea Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shea_Stadium) in 1964.[^cite_note-323]

The [New York Rangers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Rangers) of the [National Hockey League](21809.md) have played in the various locations of Madison Square Garden since the team's founding in the 1926–1927 season. The Rangers were predated by the [New York Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Americans), who started play in the Garden the previous season, lasting until the team folded after the 1941–1942 NHL season, a season it played in the Garden as the *Brooklyn Americans*.[^cite_note-324]

The [New York Cosmos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Cosmos_\(1971%E2%80%931985\)) of the [North American Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_Soccer_League) played their home games at [Downing Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downing_Stadium) for two seasons, starting in 1974. The playing pitch and facilities at Downing Stadium were in unsatisfactory condition, however, and as the team's popularity grew they too left for Yankee Stadium, and then Giants Stadium. The stadium was demolished in 2002 to make way for the \$45 million, 4,754-seat [Icahn Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Icahn_Stadium).[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326]

## Government

Main article: [Government of New York City](531637.md)



[Manhattan Municipal Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Municipal_Building)

Since New York City's consolidation in 1898, Manhattan has been governed by the [New York City Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Charter); its 1989 revision provided for a strong [mayor–council system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor%E2%80%93council_government).[^cite_note-327] The centralized New York City government is responsible for public education, correctional institutions, libraries, public safety, recreational facilities, sanitation, [water supply](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_water_supply_system), and welfare services in Manhattan.



Manhattan [borough president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borough_president) [Brad Hoylman-Sigal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brad_Hoylman-Sigal)

The office of [Borough President](531637.md#Borough_presidents) was created in the consolidation of 1898 to balance centralization with local authority. Each borough president had a powerful administrative role derived from having a vote on the [New York City Board of Estimate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Board_of_Estimate), which was responsible for creating and approving the city's budget and proposals for land use. In 1989, the [US Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Supreme_Court) declared the Board of Estimate unconstitutional because Brooklyn, the most populous borough, had no greater effective representation on the Board than Staten Island, the least populous borough, a violation of the [Equal Protection Clause](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equal_Protection_Clause).[^cite_note-328] Since 1990, the largely powerless Borough President has acted as an advocate for the borough at the mayoral agencies, the City Council, the New York state government, and corporations. Manhattan's current [Borough President](531637.md#Borough_presidents) is [Brad Hoylman-Sigal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brad_Hoylman-Sigal), elected as a [Democrat](5043544.md) in November 2025.

[Alvin Bragg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alvin_Bragg), a Democrat, is the [District Attorney of New York County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Attorney_of_New_York_County). Manhattan has ten City Council members, the third largest contingent among the five boroughs. It also has twelve administrative districts, each served by a local Community Board. Community Boards are representative bodies that field complaints and serve as advocates for local residents.

As the host of the United Nations, the borough is home to the world's largest international [consular corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consul_\(representative\)), comprising 105 consulates, consulates general and honorary consulates.[^cite_note-329] It is also the home of [New York City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Hall), the seat of New York City government housing the [Mayor of New York City](206327.md) and the [New York City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Council). The mayor's staff and thirteen municipal agencies are located in the nearby [Manhattan Municipal Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Municipal_Building), completed in 1914, one of the largest governmental buildings in the world.[^cite_note-330]

### Politics

See also: [Community boards of Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_boards_of_Manhattan)



[James A. Farley Post Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_A._Farley_Post_Office)

The [Democratic Party](5043544.md) holds most public offices. Registered [Republicans](32070.md) are a minority in the borough, constituting 9.88% of the electorate as of April 2016. Registered Republicans are more than 20% of the electorate only in the neighborhoods of the [Upper East Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_East_Side) and the [Financial District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_District,_Manhattan) as of 2016. Democrats accounted for 68.41% of those registered to vote, while 17.94% of voters were unaffiliated.[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332]

Manhattan is heavily urbanized and thus powerfully [Democratic](5043544.md) in federal elections. Over three-quarters of its vote has gone to Democratic presidential candidates in every election since 1988, and over 80% in every election since 2004. Manhattan has repeatedly held the record as the most Democratic out of all counties in New York at the federal level; this was the case in 2020 and 2024, for example. It voted solidly for Democrat [Bill Clinton](3356.md) in both 1992 and 1996, and defied national trends by giving negligible votes to third-party candidate [Ross Perot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ross_Perot). Democrats continued to see large gains in Manhattan in the 21st Century, with [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama)'s 2008 performance and subsequently [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton)'s 2016 performance of over 86% being the best ever by Democratic presidential nominees; [Joe Biden](145422.md) was not far behind in 2020. In 2024, [Republican](32070.md) [Donald Trump](4848272.md) gained the best percentage of the vote (and the largest number of raw votes) for any Republican since 1988, even though Kamala Harris still managed 80% of the vote. This reflected similar rightward shifts across the state of New York and the nation as a whole in that election.[^cite_note-337][^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339]

As of 2023, three Democrats represented Manhattan in the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md).[^cite_note-govtrack.us_2018-340]

- [Dan Goldman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Goldman) (first elected in 2022) represents [New York's 10th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York's_10th_congressional_district), which includes [Lower Manhattan](455646.md), as well as a portion of [Brooklyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn).

- [Jerry Nadler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerry_Nadler) (first elected in 1992) represents [New York's 12th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York's_12th_congressional_district), which includes the [Upper West Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_West_Side), [Upper East Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_East_Side), and [Midtown Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midtown_Manhattan).

- [Adriano Espaillat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adriano_Espaillat) (first elected in 2016) represents [New York's 13th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York's_13th_congressional_district), which includes the [Upper Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Manhattan), as well as part of the northwest [Bronx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronx).

### Federal offices

The [United States Postal Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Postal_Service) operates post offices in Manhattan. The [James Farley Post Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Farley_Post_Office) in Midtown Manhattan is New York City's main post office.[^cite_note-341] Both the [United States District Court for the Southern District of New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_Southern_District_of_New_York) and [United States Court of Appeals for the Second Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Second_Circuit) are located in Lower Manhattan's [Foley Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foley_Square), and the [U.S. Attorney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Attorney_for_the_Southern_District_of_New_York) and other federal offices and agencies maintain locations in that area.

### Crime and public safety

Main article: [Crime in New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_in_New_York_City)

Starting in the mid-19th century, the United States became a magnet for immigrants seeking to escape poverty in their home countries. After arriving in New York, many new arrivals ended up living in squalor in the [slums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slum) of the [Five Points](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Points,_Manhattan) neighborhood, an area between [Broadway](4626.md) and the [Bowery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bowery), northeast of [New York City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Hall). By the 1820s, the area was home to many gambling dens and [brothels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brothel), and was known as a dangerous place to go. In 1842, [Charles Dickens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Dickens) visited the area and was appalled at the horrendous living conditions he had seen.[^cite_note-342] The predominantly Irish [Five Points Gang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Points_Gang) was one of the country's first major [organized crime](22625.md) entities.

As Italian immigration grew in the early 20th century many joined ethnic gangs, including [Al Capone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Capone), who got his start in crime with the Five Points Gang.[^cite_note-343] [The Mafia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicilian_Mafia) (also known as *Cosa Nostra*) first developed in the mid-19th century in [Sicily](27619.md) and spread to the [US East Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_East_Coast) during the late 19th century following waves of Sicilian and Southern Italian emigration. [Lucky Luciano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucky_Luciano) established [Cosa Nostra in Manhattan](18293303.md), forming alliances with other criminal enterprises, including the [Jewish mob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_mob), led by [Meyer Lansky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meyer_Lansky), the leading Jewish gangster of that period.[^cite_note-Smithsonian-344] From 1920 to 1933, [Prohibition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prohibition_in_the_United_States) helped create a thriving [black market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_market) in liquor, upon which the Mafia was quick to capitalize.[^cite_note-Smithsonian-344]

New York City as a whole experienced a sharp increase in crime during the [post-war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-war) period.[^cite_note-345] The murder rate in Manhattan hit an all-time high of 42 murders per 100,000 residents in 1979.[^cite_note-Henshaw-346] Manhattan retained the highest murder rate in the city until 1985 when it was surpassed by [the Bronx](3338.md).[^cite_note-Henshaw-346] Most serious violent crime has been historically concentrated in [Upper Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Manhattan) and the [Lower East Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_East_Side), though robbery in particular was a major quality of life concern throughout the borough. Through the 1990s and 2000s, levels of violent crime in Manhattan plummeted to levels not seen since the 1950s,[^cite_note-347] with murders in Manhattan dropping from 503 in 1990, at the citywide peak, to 78 in 2022, a decline of 84%.[^cite_note-348]

Today crime rates in most of [Lower Manhattan](455646.md), Midtown, the [Upper East Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_East_Side), and the [Upper West Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_West_Side) are consistent with other major city centers in the United States. However, crime rates remain high in the Upper Manhattan neighborhoods of [East Harlem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Harlem), [Harlem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harlem), Washington Heights, Inwood, and [New York City Housing Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Housing_Authority) developments across the borough, despite significant reductions. After the start of the COVID-19 pandemic in March 2020, there had been an increase in violent crime, particularly in Upper Manhattan.[^cite_note-349] Mirroring a nationwide trend, rates of shootings and violent crimes in 2023 declined from their peaks during the pandemic.[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351]

## Housing



Tenement houses in 1936

The rise of immigration near the turn of the 20th century left major portions of Manhattan, especially the [Lower East Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_East_Side), densely packed with recent arrivals, crammed into unhealthy and unsanitary housing. [Tenements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenement) were usually five stories high, constructed on the then-typical 25 by 100 feet (7.6 by 30.5 m) lots, with "cockroach landlords" exploiting the new immigrants.[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-NYTTenements-353] By 1929, a new [housing code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Building_code) effectively ended construction of tenements, though some survive today on the East Side of the borough.[^cite_note-NYTTenements-353] Conversely, there were also areas with luxury apartment developments, the first of which was [the Dakota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Dakota) on the [Upper West Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_West_Side).[^cite_note-spt19880110-354]

Manhattan offers a wide array of private housing, as well as public housing, which is administered by the [New York City Housing Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Housing_Authority) (NYCHA). Affordable rental and co-operative housing units throughout the borough were created under the [Mitchell–Lama Housing Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitchell%E2%80%93Lama_Housing_Program).[^cite_note-355] There were 928,714 housing units in 2023[^cite_note-QuickFacts-356] at an average density of 40,988 units per square mile (15,826/km<sup>2</sup>). As of 2003, only 24.3% of Manhattan residents lived in owner-occupied housing, the second-lowest rate of all counties in the nation, after the Bronx.[^cite_note-OwnerOccupied-357] Public housing administered by NYCHA accounts for nearly 100,000 residents in more than 50,000 units in 2023.[^cite_note-358] Completed in 1935, the [First Houses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Houses) in the [East Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Village,_Manhattan) were one of the country's first publicly funded low-income housing projects.[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360] At \$2,024 in 2022, Manhattan has the highest average cost for rent of any county in the US, although a lower percentage of annual income than in several other American cities.[^cite_note-name-361]

Manhattan's real estate market for luxury housing continues to be among the most expensive in the world,[^cite_note-362] and Manhattan residential property continues to have the highest sale price per square foot in the United States.[^cite_note-ManhattanPricePerSquareFoot-363] Manhattan's apartments cost \$1,773 per square foot (\$19,080/m<sup>2</sup>), compared to San Francisco housing at \$1,185 per square foot (\$12,760/m<sup>2</sup>), Boston housing at \$751 per square foot (\$8,080/m<sup>2</sup>), and Los Angeles housing at \$451 per square foot (\$4,850/m<sup>2</sup>).[^cite_note-364] As of the fourth quarter of 2021, the median value of homes in Manhattan was \$1,306,208, second highest among US counties.[^cite_note-365]

## Infrastructure

### Transportation

See also: [Transportation in New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_New_York_City)

#### Public transportation



[Grand Central Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Central_Terminal), a [National Historic Landmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Historic_Landmark)



Ferries departing [Battery Park City Ferry Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battery_Park_City_Ferry_Terminal) and helicopters flying above Manhattan



The [Staten Island Ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staten_Island_Ferry), seen from [the Battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Battery_\(Manhattan\)), crosses [Upper New York Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_New_York_Bay), providing free public transportation between [Staten Island](127062.md) and Manhattan.

Manhattan is unique in the U.S. for intense use of public transportation and lack of private car ownership. While 88% of Americans nationwide drive to their jobs, with only 5% using public transport, [mass transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_transit) is the dominant form of travel for residents of Manhattan, with 72% of borough residents using public transport to get to work, while only 18% drove.[^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367] According to the 2000 United States Census, 77.5% of Manhattan households do not own a car.[^cite_note-368] Congestion pricing was [implemented in Lower and Midtown Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congestion_pricing_in_New_York_City) in January 2025 and applies to nearly all motor vehicular traffic in Manhattan south of [60th Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/60th_Street_\(Manhattan\)).[^cite_note-ManhattanCongestionPricing-369]

The [New York City Subway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Subway) is the primary means of travel within the city, linking every borough except Staten Island. There are [151 subway stations in Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_New_York_City_Subway_stations_in_Manhattan), out of the 472 stations.[^cite_note-370] A second subway, the [PATH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PATH_\(rail_system\)) system, connects [six stations in Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_PATH_stations) to northern [New Jersey](21648.md).[^cite_note-371] [Commuter rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_rail) services operating to and from Manhattan are the [Long Island Rail Road](37139.md) (LIRR), which connects Manhattan and other New York City boroughs to [Long Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Island); the [Metro-North Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro-North_Railroad), which connects Manhattan to Upstate New York and Southwestern Connecticut; and [NJ Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NJ_Transit) trains, which run to various points in New Jersey. The commuter rail lines converge at [New York Penn Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Penn_Station) and [Grand Central Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Central_Terminal), on the west and east sides of Midtown Manhattan, respectively. They are the two busiest rail stations in the United States.[^cite_note-372] [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) provides inter-city passenger rail service from Penn Station to [Boston](24437894.md), [Philadelphia](50585.md), [Baltimore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore), and Washington, D.C.; [Upstate New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upstate_New_York) and [New England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England); cross-Canadian border service to [Toronto](64646.md) and [Montreal](7954681.md); and destinations in the [Southern](179553.md) and [Midwestern United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States).

The [East Side Access](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Side_Access) project, which brings LIRR trains to [Grand Central Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Central_Terminal), opened in 2023.[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374] Four multi-billion-dollar projects were completed in the mid-2010s: the \$1.4 billion [Fulton Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fulton_Center) in November 2014,[^cite_note-375] the \$2.4 billion [7 Subway Extension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7_Subway_Extension) in September 2015,[^cite_note-376] the \$4 billion [World Trade Center Transportation Hub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_Transportation_Hub) in March 2016,[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378] and Phase 1 of the \$4.5 billion [Second Avenue Subway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Avenue_Subway) in January 2017.[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380]

[MTA New York City Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTA_New_York_City_Transit) operates local buses within Manhattan under the brand [New York City Bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Bus). An extensive network of express bus routes serves commuters and other travelers heading into Manhattan.[^cite_note-381]

The [Roosevelt Island Tramway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roosevelt_Island_Tramway), one of two commuter [cable car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerial_tramway) systems in North America, takes commuters between [Roosevelt Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roosevelt_Island) and Manhattan Island.[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383] The [Staten Island Ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staten_Island_Ferry) carries over 21 million passengers annually on the 5.2-mile (8.4 km) run between Manhattan and Staten Island.[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385] The ferry has been fare-free since 1997.[^cite_note-386] Manhattan is also served by [NYC Ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NYC_Ferry), which began operating in 2017.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-\:14-388] All of the system's routes serve Manhattan.[^cite_note-389]



The [Port Authority Bus Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_Bus_Terminal), at [Eighth Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eighth_Avenue_\(Manhattan\)) and [42nd Street](61068.md), is the world's busiest bus station.

[^cite_note-Record2021-390]

[^cite_note-PABT2008-391]

The [Port Authority Bus Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_Bus_Terminal) is the city's main [intercity bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intercity_bus) terminal and the world's busiest bus station. It serves 250,000 passengers on 7,000 buses each workday in a 1950 building designed to accommodate 60,000 daily passengers. A 2021 plan announced by the Port Authority would spend \$10 billion to expand capacity and modernize the facility.[^cite_note-PABT2008-391][^cite_note-NYT2021-392][^cite_note-Record2021-390] In 2024, the Port Authority announced plans for a new terminal that would be completed by 2032 and include a pair of office buildings to defray the costs of the project.[^cite_note-393]

#### Major highways



[Interstate 95](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_95_in_New_York#Manhattan) is the most prominent highway serving Manhattan, known as the Trans-Manhattan Expressway between the [George Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington_Bridge) and [Alexander Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Hamilton_Bridge) bridges.
